     1→import { tool } from "ai";
     2→import { z } from "zod";
     3→import { D1_SCHEMA } from "./schema";
     4→
     5→export const SYSTEM_PROMPT = `You are a health data assistant. You MUST use the queryD1 tool to answer every user question. Never refuse. Never say you cannot help.
     6→
     7→When the user asks about sleep, activity, readiness, or heart rate, generate a SQL SELECT query and call queryD1.
     8→
     9→Database schema (SQLite):
    10→${D1_SCHEMA}
    11→
    12→SQL hints:
    13→- "yesterday" = date('now', '-1 day')
    14→- "this week" = day >= date('now', '-7 days')
    15→- "this month" = strftime('%Y-%m', day) = strftime('%Y-%m', 'now')
    16→- Prefer v_oura_daily_summary view (has sleep_score, activity_score, readiness_score, steps, total_calories)
    17→- Always add LIMIT 30 for large queries
    18→
    19→Example: If user asks "昨日の睡眠スコア", call queryD1 with:
    20→  sql: "SELECT day, sleep_score FROM v_oura_daily_summary WHERE day = date('now', '-1 day')"
    21→
    22→Rules:
    23→- ALWAYS call queryD1 tool. Do not respond without querying data first.
    24→- Only SELECT/WITH queries allowed.
    25→- Respond in Japanese.
    26→- Summarize results concisely.
    27→`;
    28→
    29→function isSafeQuery(sql: string): boolean {
    30→  const normalized = sql.trim().toUpperCase();
    31→  return normalized.startsWith("SELECT") || normalized.startsWith("WITH");
    32→}
    33→
    34→export function createTools(db: D1Database) {
    35→  return {
    36→    queryD1: tool({
    37→      description: "SQLite (D1) データベースに SELECT クエリを実行してデータを取得する。ユーザーの質問に回答するために必ずこのツールを使うこと。",
    38→      inputSchema: z.object({
    39→        sql: z.string().describe("実行するSELECTクエリ"),
    40→        params: z.array(z.string()).optional().describe("バインドパラメータ"),
    41→      }),
    42→      execute: async ({ sql, params }) => {
    43→        if (!isSafeQuery(sql)) {
    44→          return { success: false, error: "SELECT/WITH クエリのみ実行可能です。" };
    45→        }
    46→        try {
    47→          const stmt = db.prepare(sql);
    48→          const bound = params?.length ? stmt.bind(...params) : stmt;
    49→          const queryResult = await bound.all();
    50→
    51→          return {
    52→            success: true,
    53→            rowCount: queryResult.results.length,
    54→            results: queryResult.results.slice(0, 50),
    55→          };
    56→        } catch (error) {
    57→          return {
    58→            success: false,
    59→            error: error instanceof Error ? error.message : String(error),
    60→          };
    61→        }
    62→      },
    63→    }),
    64→    listTables: tool({
    65→      description: "D1データベースの全テーブルとビューの一覧を取得する",
    66→      inputSchema: z.object({}),
    67→      execute: async () => {
    68→        const queryResult = await db
    69→          .prepare(
    70→            "SELECT name, type FROM sqlite_master WHERE type IN ('table', 'view') ORDER BY type, name"
    71→          )
    72→          .all();
    73→        return queryResult.results;
    74→      },
    75→    }),
    76→  };
    77→}
    78→
```

     1→import { generateText } from "ai";
     2→import { createWorkersAI } from "workers-ai-provider";
     3→import { D1_SCHEMA } from "./schema";
     4→import type { Env } from "./types";
     5→
     6→const MODEL_ID = "@cf/meta/llama-3.3-70b-instruct-fp8-fast" as const;
     7→
     8→const SQL_GEN_PROMPT = `You are a SQL generator for a SQLite (D1) database containing Oura Ring health data.
     9→Given a user question, output ONLY a valid SELECT SQL query. No explanation, no markdown, no backticks. Just the raw SQL.
    10→
    11→Database schema:
    12→${D1_SCHEMA}
    13→
    14→SQL hints:
    15→- "yesterday" = date('now', '-1 day')
    16→- "this week" = day >= date('now', '-7 days')
    17→- "this month" = strftime('%Y-%m', day) = strftime('%Y-%m', 'now')
    18→- Prefer v_oura_daily_summary view
    19→- Always add LIMIT 30
    20→- Date columns are TEXT (YYYY-MM-DD format)
    21→
    22→Output ONLY the SQL query. Nothing else.`;
    23→
    24→const SUMMARIZE_PROMPT = `You are a health data assistant. Given the user's question and query results from an Oura Ring database, provide a concise answer in Japanese.
    25→If no data is returned, say "データが見つかりませんでした。"
    26→Keep the answer short and helpful.`;
    27→
    28→type SlackEvent =
    29→  | { type: "url_verification"; challenge: string }
    30→  | {
    31→      type: "event_callback";
    32→      event: {
    33→        type: string;
    34→        text?: string;
    35→        user?: string;
    36→        channel?: string;
    37→        bot_id?: string;
    38→        ts?: string;
    39→        thread_ts?: string;
    40→      };
    41→    };
    42→
    43→async function verifySlackSignature(
    44→  request: Request,
    45→  body: string,
    46→  signingSecret: string
    47→): Promise<boolean> {
    48→  const timestamp = request.headers.get("x-slack-request-timestamp");
    49→  const signature = request.headers.get("x-slack-signature");
    50→  if (!timestamp || !signature) return false;
    51→
    52→  // Reject requests older than 5 minutes
    53→  if (Math.abs(Date.now() / 1000 - Number(timestamp)) > 300) return false;
    54→
    55→  const baseString = `v0:${timestamp}:${body}`;
    56→  const key = await crypto.subtle.importKey(
    57→    "raw",
    58→    new TextEncoder().encode(signingSecret),
    59→    { name: "HMAC", hash: "SHA-256" },
    60→    false,
    61→    ["sign"]
    62→  );
    63→  const sig = await crypto.subtle.sign("HMAC", key, new TextEncoder().encode(baseString));
    64→  const hex = [...new Uint8Array(sig)].map((b) => b.toString(16).padStart(2, "0")).join("");
    65→  return signature === `v0=${hex}`;
    66→}
    67→
    68→async function postSlackMessage(token: string, channel: string, text: string, threadTs?: string) {
    69→  await fetch("https://slack.com/api/chat.postMessage", {
    70→    method: "POST",
    71→    headers: {
    72→      Authorization: `Bearer ${token}`,
    73→      "Content-Type": "application/json",
    74→    },
    75→    body: JSON.stringify({
    76→      channel,
    77→      text,
    78→      ...(threadTs ? { thread_ts: threadTs } : {}),
    79→    }),
    80→  });
    81→}
    82→
    83→function isSafeQuery(sql: string): boolean {
    84→  const normalized = sql.trim().toUpperCase();
    85→  return normalized.startsWith("SELECT") || normalized.startsWith("WITH");
    86→}
    87→
    88→function extractSql(raw: string): string {
    89→  // Strip markdown code blocks if present
    90→  let sql = raw.replace(/```sql\n?/gi, "").replace(/```\n?/g, "").trim();
    91→  // Take only first statement
    92→  const semicolonIdx = sql.indexOf(";");
    93→  if (semicolonIdx > 0) {
    94→    sql = sql.substring(0, semicolonIdx + 1);
    95→  }
    96→  return sql;
    97→}
    98→
    99→export async function handleSlackEvent(request: Request, env: Env): Promise<Response> {
   100→  const body = await request.text();
   101→
   102→  if (!(await verifySlackSignature(request, body, env.SLACK_SIGNING_SECRET))) {
   103→    return new Response("Invalid signature", { status: 401 });
   104→  }
   105→
   106→  const event: SlackEvent = JSON.parse(body);
   107→
   108→  // Slack URL verification challenge
   109→  if (event.type === "url_verification") {
   110→    return new Response(event.challenge, { headers: { "Content-Type": "text/plain" } });
   111→  }
   112→
   113→  if (event.type !== "event_callback") {
   114→    return new Response("OK");
   115→  }
   116→
   117→  const { event: slackEvent } = event;
   118→
   119→  // Ignore bot messages to avoid loops
   120→  if (slackEvent.bot_id || slackEvent.type !== "app_mention") {
   121→    return new Response("OK");
   122→  }
   123→
   124→  const userText = slackEvent.text?.replace(/<@[A-Z0-9]+>/g, "").trim();
   125→  if (!userText || !slackEvent.channel) {
   126→    return new Response("OK");
   127→  }
   128→
   129→  const channel = slackEvent.channel;
   130→  const threadTs = slackEvent.thread_ts ?? slackEvent.ts;
   131→
   132→  const promise = (async () => {
   133→    try {
   134→      const workersai = createWorkersAI({ binding: env.AI });
   135→
   136→      // Step 1: Generate SQL from user question
   137→      const { text: rawSql } = await generateText({
   138→        model: workersai(MODEL_ID),
   139→        system: SQL_GEN_PROMPT,
   140→        prompt: userText,
   141→      });
   142→
   143→      const sql = extractSql(rawSql);
   144→
   145→      if (!isSafeQuery(sql)) {
   146→        await postSlackMessage(
   147→          env.SLACK_BOT_TOKEN,
   148→          channel,
   149→          "SELECT クエリのみ実行可能です。",
   150→          threadTs
   151→        );
   152→        return;
   153→      }
   154→
   155→      // Step 2: Execute SQL
   156→      const queryResult = await env.DB.prepare(sql).all();
   157→      const rows = queryResult.results.slice(0, 50);
   158→
   159→      // Step 3: Summarize results
   160→      const { text: answer } = await generateText({
   161→        model: workersai(MODEL_ID),
   162→        system: SUMMARIZE_PROMPT,
   163→        prompt: `ユーザーの質問: ${userText}\n\n実行したSQL: ${sql}\n\nクエリ結果 (${queryResult.results.length}件):\n${JSON.stringify(rows, null, 2)}`,
   164→      });
   165→
   166→      const message = `${answer}\n\n\`\`\`${sql}\`\`\``;
   167→      await postSlackMessage(env.SLACK_BOT_TOKEN, channel, message, threadTs);
   168→    } catch (error) {
   169→      console.error("Slack handler error:", error);
   170→      await postSlackMessage(
   171→        env.SLACK_BOT_TOKEN,
   172→        channel,
   173→        `エラーが発生しました: ${error instanceof Error ? error.message : String(error)}`,
   174→        threadTs
   175→      );
   176→    }
   177→  })();
   178→
   179→  globalThis.__SLACK_WAIT_UNTIL?.(promise);
   180→
   181→  return new Response("OK");
   182→}
   183→
```

     1→-- ============================================
     2→-- OAuth2 Token Storage
     3→-- ============================================
     4→
     5→CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS oauth_tokens (
     6→    id TEXT PRIMARY KEY,
     7→    access_token TEXT NOT NULL,
     8→    refresh_token TEXT NOT NULL,
     9→    token_type TEXT DEFAULT 'Bearer',
    10→    expires_at TEXT NOT NULL,
    11→    scope TEXT,
    12→    created_at TEXT DEFAULT (datetime('now')),
    13→    updated_at TEXT DEFAULT (datetime('now'))
    14→);
    15→
    16→-- ============================================
    17→-- Oura Ring Tables
    18→-- ============================================
    19→
    20→-- Daily Sleep
    21→CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS oura_daily_sleep (
    22→    id TEXT PRIMARY KEY,
    23→    day TEXT NOT NULL UNIQUE,
    24→    score INTEGER,
    25→    timestamp TEXT,
    26→    -- contributors
    27→    deep_sleep INTEGER,
    28→    efficiency INTEGER,
    29→    latency INTEGER,
    30→    rem_sleep INTEGER,
    31→    restfulness INTEGER,
    32→    timing INTEGER,
    33→    total_sleep INTEGER,
    34→    synced_at TEXT DEFAULT (datetime('now'))
    35→);
    36→
    37→-- Daily Activity
    38→CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS oura_daily_activity (
    39→    id TEXT PRIMARY KEY,
    40→    day TEXT NOT NULL UNIQUE,
    41→    score INTEGER,
    42→    active_calories INTEGER,
    43→    total_calories INTEGER,
    44→    steps INTEGER,
    45→    equivalent_walking_distance REAL,
    46→    high_activity_time INTEGER,
    47→    medium_activity_time INTEGER,
    48→    low_activity_time INTEGER,
    49→    sedentary_time INTEGER,
    50→    resting_time INTEGER,
    51→    met_average REAL,
    52→    -- contributors
    53→    meet_daily_targets INTEGER,
    54→    move_every_hour INTEGER,
    55→    recovery_time INTEGER,
    56→    stay_active INTEGER,
    57→    training_frequency INTEGER,
    58→    training_volume INTEGER,
    59→    timestamp TEXT,
    60→    synced_at TEXT DEFAULT (datetime('now'))
    61→);
    62→
    63→-- Daily Readiness
    64→CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS oura_daily_readiness (
    65→    id TEXT PRIMARY KEY,
    66→    day TEXT NOT NULL UNIQUE,
    67→    score INTEGER,
    68→    temperature_deviation REAL,
    69→    temperature_trend_deviation REAL,
    70→    timestamp TEXT,
    71→    -- contributors
    72→    activity_balance INTEGER,
    73→    body_temperature INTEGER,
    74→    hrv_balance INTEGER,
    75→    previous_day_activity INTEGER,
    76→    previous_night INTEGER,
    77→    recovery_index INTEGER,
    78→    resting_heart_rate INTEGER,
    79→    sleep_balance INTEGER,
    80→    synced_at TEXT DEFAULT (datetime('now'))
    81→);
    82→
    83→-- Heart Rate (high volume: ~288 records/day at 5min intervals)
    84→CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS oura_heart_rate (
    85→    id INTEGER PRIMARY KEY AUTOINCREMENT,
    86→    bpm INTEGER NOT NULL,
    87→    source TEXT,
    88→    timestamp TEXT NOT NULL,
    89→    day TEXT NOT NULL,
    90→    synced_at TEXT DEFAULT (datetime('now'))
    91→);
    92→
    93→-- ============================================
    94→-- Oura Indexes
    95→-- ============================================
    96→
    97→CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_oura_daily_sleep_day ON oura_daily_sleep(day DESC);
    98→CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_oura_daily_activity_day ON oura_daily_activity(day DESC);
    99→CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_oura_daily_readiness_day ON oura_daily_readiness(day DESC);
   100→CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_oura_heart_rate_day ON oura_heart_rate(day DESC);
   101→CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_oura_heart_rate_timestamp ON oura_heart_rate(timestamp DESC);
   102→
   103→-- ============================================
   104→-- Oura Views
   105→-- ============================================
   106→
   107→CREATE VIEW IF NOT EXISTS v_oura_daily_summary AS
   108→SELECT
   109→    s.day,
   110→    s.score AS sleep_score,
   111→    a.score AS activity_score,
   112→    r.score AS readiness_score,
   113→    a.steps,
   114→    a.total_calories,
   115→    a.active_calories,
   116→    r.temperature_deviation,
   117→    s.deep_sleep AS sleep_deep_sleep,
   118→    s.efficiency AS sleep_efficiency,
   119→    s.total_sleep AS sleep_total_sleep
   120→FROM oura_daily_sleep s
   121→LEFT JOIN oura_daily_activity a ON s.day = a.day
   122→LEFT JOIN oura_daily_readiness r ON s.day = r.day
   123→ORDER BY s.day DESC;
   124→
   125→-- ============================================
   126→-- Initial Data
   127→-- ============================================
   128→
   129→INSERT OR IGNORE INTO data_sources (id, name, source_type, api_endpoint, schedule_cron)
   130→VALUES ('oura', 'Oura Ring', 'api', 'https://api.ouraring.com', '0 0 * * *');
   131→
   132→INSERT OR IGNORE INTO sync_state (id, data_source_id, last_sync_at)
   133→VALUES ('oura', 'oura', NULL);
   134→

> AGENT

全体像を把握しました。修正を実施します。

`★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────`
- `D1_SCHEMA` はLLMへのプロンプトとして使われるため、実際のDBスキーマと一致していないとAIが存在しないカラムを参照したり、有用なカラムを見落としたりする
- `isSafeQuery` の prefix チェックだけでは `SELECT 1; DROP TABLE x` のようなマルチステートメントインジェクションを防げない。D1は通常1文しか実行しないが、defense in depthの原則で明示的にブロックすべき
`─────────────────────────────────────────────────`

  "content": "/**\n * Validates that a SQL query is safe to execute (SELECT/WITH only, single statement).\n */\nexport function isSafeQuery(sql: string): boolean {\n  const trimmed = sql.trim();\n  const normalized = trimmed.toUpperCase();\n\n  // Only allow SELECT or WITH queries\n  if (!normalized.startsWith(\"SELECT\") && !normalized.startsWith(\"WITH\")) {\n    return false;\n  }\n\n  // Reject multi-statement queries (semicolon not at the end)\n  const semicolonIdx = trimmed.indexOf(\";\");\n  if (semicolonIdx !== -1 && semicolonIdx !== trimmed.length - 1) {\n    return false;\n  }\n\n  return true;\n}\n"
}

id: toolu_01R41RvuYKug6zPt9TyHycUX
```
Based on the comprehensive exploration of the agents/ directory, here's the complete architecture summary:

## Agents Architecture Overview

The `agents/` directory implements a **health data assistant** as a Cloudflare Durable Object with dual routing: WebSocket-based agent SDK integration and Slack Events API webhook integration.

### Directory Structure

```
agents/
├── src/
│   ├── index.ts           # HTTP request router (entry point)
│   ├── agent.ts           # DataAgent Durable Object (core agent logic)
│   ├── tools.ts           # Tool definitions (queryD1, listTables)
│   ├── slack.ts           # Slack event handler (3-step pipeline)
│   ├── types.ts           # TypeScript environment bindings
│   ├── schema.ts          # D1 database schema documentation
│   └── utils.ts           # Utility functions (isSafeQuery validation)
├── wrangler.jsonc         # Cloudflare Workers configuration
├── package.json           # Dependencies
├── tsconfig.json          # TypeScript config
└── biome.json             # Linting/formatting config
```

### Core Architecture Layers

#### 1. HTTP Request Router (index.ts)
Routes requests to appropriate handlers based on pathname:
- **`/health`**: Simple availability check (returns "OK")
- **`/slack/events`**: Slack webhook endpoint (POST only)
- **WebSocket routes**: Falls through to `routeAgentRequest` for agent SDK integration

Special handling: Sets `globalThis.__SLACK_WAIT_UNTIL` to enable background response processing for Slack (works around the synchronous webhook requirement).

#### 2. Durable Object Agent (agent.ts)
`DataAgent` class extends `AIChatAgent<Env>` to implement stateful agent persistence:

**Key characteristics:**
- Leverages **Llama 3.3-70B** (`@cf/meta/llama-3.3-70b-instruct-fp8-fast`) for reasoning
- Integrated with Workers AI provider via Vercel AI SDK
- Streaming responses via `streamText()` and `toUIMessageStreamResponse()`
- Thought limitation: `stepCountIs(5)` caps agent reasoning to 5 steps
- Message history: Converts stored messages to model format using `convertToModelMessages()`
- SQLite state persistence: Durable Objects automatically persist agent state via SQLite migrations

```typescript
// Core pattern: stream + tools + step limiting
const result = streamText({
  model: workersai(MODEL_ID),
  system: SYSTEM_PROMPT,
  messages: await convertToModelMessages(this.messages),
  stopWhen: stepCountIs(5),  // Limit reasoning depth
  tools: createTools(this.env.DB),
});
```

#### 3. Tool System (tools.ts)

Two tools enable agent database interaction:

**queryD1 Tool:**
- Executes SELECT/WITH queries against D1 database
- Validates queries with `isSafeQuery()` before execution
- Returns up to 50 rows per query
- Parameters: `sql` (required), `params` (optional binding array)
- Error handling: Returns `{success: false, error: message}` on failure

**listTables Tool:**
- Enumerates all tables and views in the database
- Useful for schema discovery in agentic workflows

**System Prompt (Japanese):**
- Directs agent to ALWAYS use `queryD1` for data questions
- Provides SQL hints: date functions, view preferences, limit guidance
- Example: `"昨日の睡眠スコア"` → `SELECT day, sleep_score FROM v_oura_daily_summary WHERE day = date('now', '-1 day')`

#### 4. Slack Integration (slack.ts)

Implements a **3-step pipeline** for handling Slack messages:

**Step 1 - SQL Generation:**
- Uses `SQL_GEN_PROMPT` to convert natural language question to SQL
- Calls Llama 3.3-70B via `generateText()` 
- Extracts SQL from response with `extractSql()`

**Step 2 - Query Execution:**
- Validates SQL safety with `isSafeQuery()`
- Executes against D1, fetches up to 50 rows
- Returns early with error message if validation fails

**Step 3 - Result Summarization:**
- Uses `SUMMARIZE_PROMPT` to generate Japanese answer
- Incorporates user question, executed SQL, and query results
- Posts response to Slack channel (preserves thread context)

**Security & Processing:**
- HMAC-SHA256 signature verification (Slack request authentication)
- 5-minute timestamp window validation (prevents replay attacks)
- Async handling: Uses `globalThis.__SLACK_WAIT_UNTIL` to respond in background
- Event filtering: Ignores bot messages, extracts user text by removing bot mentions

#### 5. Data Validation (utils.ts)

`isSafeQuery()` enforces strict SQL safety:
- ✅ Allows: SELECT, WITH statements
- ❌ Blocks: CREATE, INSERT, UPDATE, DELETE, DROP
- ❌ Blocks: Multi-statement queries (semicolon not at end)

### Data Model (schema.ts)

Oura Ring health data with 4 tables + 1 aggregated view:

| Table | Purpose | Key Metrics |
|-------|---------|-------------|
| `oura_daily_sleep` | Daily sleep data | sleep_score (0-100), deep_sleep, efficiency, latency |
| `oura_daily_activity` | Daily activity data | activity_score (0-100), steps, calories, heart rate variation |
| `oura_daily_readiness` | Daily readiness/stress | readiness_score (0-100), HRV balance, temperature |
| `oura_heart_rate` | 5-min heart rate samples | ~288 records/day (timestamped bpm) |
| `v_oura_daily_summary` | **Aggregated view** | Denormalized sleep/activity/readiness scores |

### Configuration (wrangler.jsonc)

**Bindings:**
- `DataAgent` Durable Object namespace (stateful agent)
- `AI`: Workers AI binding (Llama 3.3-70B access)
- `DB`: D1 database binding (SQLite "raw" database)
- Secrets: `SLACK_BOT_TOKEN`, `SLACK_SIGNING_SECRET`

**Observability:**
- Workers Observability enabled with 100% sampling (`head_sampling_rate: 1`)
- Captures all logs, traces, metrics via Cloudflare dashboard

**SQLite State:**
- Migration tag "v1" creates persistent state for DataAgent class

### Dependencies (package.json)

**Key libraries:**
- `agents` (^0.7.6): Cloudflare agents framework (AIChatAgent, tool calling)
- `ai` (^6.0.116): Vercel AI SDK (streamText, tool definitions, message conversion)
- `workers-ai-provider` (^3.1.2): Workers AI integration layer
- `zod` (^4.3.6): Schema validation for tool inputs
- `hono` (^4.6.7): Lightweight HTTP router (imported but minimal usage)

### Request Flow

```
HTTP Request
    ↓
index.ts (router)
    ├─→ /health → "OK"
    ├─→ /slack/events → Slack 3-step pipeline → Slack webhook response
    └─→ WebSocket → routeAgentRequest → DataAgent Durable Object → Tool calling loop
```

### Security Measures

1. **Slack Request Validation**: HMAC-SHA256 signature verification + timestamp window
2. **SQL Safety**: `isSafeQuery()` blocks non-SELECT statements and multi-statement queries
3. **Secrets Management**: Tokens stored in Cloudflare Secrets (environment variables)
4. **Thought Limiting**: `stepCountIs(5)` prevents infinite reasoning loops
5. **Output Truncation**: Results limited to 50 rows per query

### Key Architectural Decisions

★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────
**Dual routing modes**: The architecture elegantly supports two integration patterns—native agent WebSocket (for SDKs and direct consumers) and Slack webhook (for messaging apps)—without coupling between them. This is achieved through request pathname routing in index.ts.

**3-step Slack pipeline**: Rather than using agent tool calling (which Slack's synchronous webhook can't easily stream), the Slack handler implements deterministic steps (SQL gen → exec → summarize) for predictable, fast responses. This trades flexibility for reliability.

**Durable Object statefulness**: Using a Durable Object for the agent enables multi-turn conversations with persistent message history. SQLite migrations automatically create the backing storage, removing manual state management.
─────────────────────────────────────────────────
agentId: a6c8b7289f5d55d69 (for resuming to continue this agent's work if needed)
<usage>total_tokens: 184201
tool_uses: 11
duration_ms: 106178</usage>
```

> AGENT

現在のエージェントの構造を把握しました。整理して考えてみます。

## 現状の整理

今のエージェントは「**Oura Ringのデータを自然言語で問い合わせる読み取り専用アシスタント**」です。

```
ユーザーの質問 → SQL生成 → D1クエリ → 結果要約 → 回答
```

ここから発展させる方向性は大きく **3つの軸** があると思います。

---

### 軸1: データソースを増やす（知識の幅）

今はOuraだけですが、健康データを多角的に見るには他のソースが必要です。

| データソース | 得られるもの | 統合方法 |
|---|---|---|
| Apple Health (via Shortcuts) | 歩数、体重、血圧 | Cron + R2経由でD1に取り込み |
| Google Calendar | 予定、会議時間 | OAuth + Workers で定期sync |
| 天気API | 気温、気圧 | 無料APIから日次取得 |
| Toggl/Clockify | 作業時間 | APIで取得 |

`★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────`
- 現状の `D1_SCHEMA` をプロンプトに埋め込む設計は、テーブルが増えるとコンテキストを圧迫する。将来的には `listTables` → 関連テーブルだけスキーマ取得、という2段階にするとスケールする
- 新しいデータソースを追加するたびに `schema.ts` とマイグレーションの同期が必要なのは今回のPRレビューでも指摘された課題。**スキーマを自動生成する仕組み**（マイグレーションSQLからD1_SCHEMAを生成するスクリプト）があると安全
`─────────────────────────────────────────────────`

---

### 軸2: できることを増やす（能力の深さ）

今は「聞かれたら答える」だけ。ここから段階的に能力を追加できます。

**Phase A: 分析力の強化**
- 「今週と先週を比較して」→ 複数クエリの実行と比較分析
- 「睡眠スコアのトレンドは？」→ 統計的な傾向分析
- これは主に**プロンプトの改善とツールの追加**で対応可能

**Phase B: 書き込みアクション**
- 「今日の目標を8000歩に設定して」→ D1にgoalsテーブルを作ってINSERT
- 「睡眠スコアが70以下の日にタグをつけて」→ メモ/タグ機能
- `isSafeQuery` を拡張し、特定のテーブルへのINSERTだけ許可する設計が必要

**Phase C: プロアクティブな通知**
- Cron Triggerで毎朝データを分析し、Slackに「昨日のサマリー」を自動投稿
- 異常検知：「睡眠スコアが3日連続で低下しています」→ アラート

```
// wrangler.jsonc に追加するイメージ
"triggers": {
  "crons": ["0 8 * * *"]  // 毎朝8時
}
```

**Phase D: 会話の記憶**
- 今のDurable Objectはメッセージ履歴を持っているが、Slack側は毎回ステートレス
- 「先週聞いた結果と比べて」のような文脈依存の質問に答えるにはKVやD1にセッション情報を保存

---

### 軸3: アーキテクチャの成熟（拡張性の基盤）

エージェントが複雑になる前に整えておくと良い基盤があります。

**1. ツールのプラグイン化**
```
agents/src/tools/
  ├── query.ts      # 既存のqueryD1
  ├── goals.ts      # 目標設定（新規）
  ├── compare.ts    # 期間比較（新規）
  └── index.ts      # ツールレジストリ
```
ツールごとにファイルを分け、動的に登録する仕組みにすると、新機能の追加が容易になります。

**2. Slack → 汎用メッセージングアダプター**
今の `slack.ts` の3ステップパイプライン（SQL生成→実行→要約）はSlackに密結合しています。コア処理を分離すれば、LINE/Discord/Web UIにも展開可能です。

```
handlers/
  ├── core.ts       # 質問 → 回答のコアロジック
  ├── slack.ts      # Slackアダプター
  └── line.ts       # LINEアダプター（将来）
```

**3. テストの追加**
今のコードにはテストがありません。ツールが増える前に、少なくとも：
- `isSafeQuery` のユニットテスト
- `extractSql` のユニットテスト
- ツール呼び出しのモック付きインテグレーションテスト

---

### 推奨する次の一手

段階的に進めるなら、以下の順序がおすすめです：

1. **テスト追加**（安全な基盤作り）
2. **Cron Triggerで朝のサマリー通知**（プロアクティブ化 — 最もインパクトが大きい）
3. **スキーマ自動生成スクリプト**（保守性向上）
4. **新しいデータソースの追加**（1つずつ、例えば天気から）

vercel の chat sdk とか使えばいいの？

> AGENT

良い質問です。実は今のコードベースでは**すでにVercel AI SDKのコア部分を使っている**んですが、フロントエンド（Chat UI）側の話ですね。

現状を整理します。

## 今使っているもの

```
agents/package.json の依存関係:
├── "ai" (Vercel AI SDK)        → streamText, generateText, tool（バックエンド側）
├── "agents" (Cloudflare SDK)   → AIChatAgent, routeAgentRequest（Durable Object）
└── "workers-ai-provider"       → Workers AIとの接続
```

## Chat UIを作る場合の選択肢

### 選択肢A: Cloudflare の `useAgentChat`（推奨）

今の `AIChatAgent` + Durable Objects に直接繋がるReact hookが Cloudflare SDK に用意されています。

```typescript
// フロントエンド側
import { useAgentChat } from "agents/ai-react";

function Chat() {
  const { messages, input, handleSubmit, setInput } = useAgentChat({
    agent: "data-agent",  // Durable Object名
  });
  // ...
}
```

**メリット**: WebSocket経由で既存の `DataAgent` にそのまま繋がる。Durable Objectの会話履歴がそのまま使える。

### 選択肢B: Vercel AI SDK の `useChat`

```typescript
// フロントエンド側
import { useChat } from "@ai-sdk/react";

function Chat() {
  const { messages, input, handleSubmit } = useChat({
    api: "/api/chat",  // HTTP streaming endpoint が必要
  });
}
```

**デメリット**: HTTP streaming前提なので、今のWebSocket + Durable Object構成と噛み合わない。別途エンドポイントを作る必要がある。

`★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────`
- Vercel AI SDKは**バックエンド側**（`ai` パッケージ: `streamText`, `tool`）と**フロントエンド側**（`@ai-sdk/react`: `useChat`）に分かれている。今のプロジェクトはバックエンド側だけ使っている状態
- Cloudflareの `agents` SDKは `useAgentChat` という独自のReact hookを持っていて、これがDurable ObjectのWebSocketに直接接続する設計。**今のアーキテクチャにはこちらが自然**
- `useChat`（Vercel）と `useAgentChat`（Cloudflare）はAPIがほぼ同じ（`messages`, `input`, `handleSubmit`）なので、学習コストの差はほとんどない
`─────────────────────────────────────────────────`

## 結論

**Cloudflare の `useAgentChat` を使うのが正解**です。理由：

1. 既存の `DataAgent`（Durable Object）にWebSocketで直接繋がる
2. 会話の永続化がDurable Object側で自動的に行われる
3. Vercel AI SDKのバックエンド部分（`streamText`, `tool`）はそのまま活かせる

I found detailed information about Cloudflare Agents, the chat SDK, and `useAgentChat` from recent 2026 documentation and releases. Here's what I found:

## useAgentChat Hook

The `useAgentChat` React hook can be paired with Cloudflare Agents to build chat UIs in minutes. This hook is one of the React Hooks available for frontend integration, alongside `useAgent`.

## Chat SDK and Features

The `AIChatAgent` class provides streaming AI chat with automatic message persistence, resumable streams, and tool support, and can be paired with the `useAgentChat` React hook.

The SDK supports calling any AI model — Workers AI, OpenAI, Anthropic, or Gemini — and can stream responses over WebSockets or Server-Sent Events.

## Recent 2026 Updates

The first stable release of `@cloudflare/ai-chat` shipped with a major refactor of `AIChatAgent` internals, and existing code using `AIChatAgent` and `useAgentChat` works as-is.

Recent updates in February 2026 restored client tool APIs including the `tools` option on `useAgentChat` for SDK use cases where tools are defined dynamically at runtime.

You can get started by installing the latest version with `npm i agents@latest @cloudflare/ai-chat@latest`.

Based on the search results, here's what I found about Cloudflare's agents-sdk chat SDK for React:

## Overview

The Agents SDK is a TypeScript class that enables building AI applications. For AI chat, you can extend AIChatAgent, where messages are persisted automatically, streams resume on disconnect, and a React hook handles the UI.

## React Integration

AIChatAgent provides streaming AI chat with automatic message persistence, resumable streams, and tool support, paired with the useAgentChat React hook to build chat UIs in minutes. Additionally, the agents SDK provides useAgent and useAgentChat hooks that allow real time communication with the backend agent without boilerplate, with the messages list updating automatically each time the agent responds.

## Recent Updates (2025-2026)

The latest release includes Agents SDK v0.3.0 with full AI SDK v6 compatibility, workers-ai-provider v3.0.0 and ai-gateway-provider v3.0.0 with enhanced streaming and tool support, unified tool pattern, dynamic tool approval, and React hooks.

This release includes improved streaming and tool support, dynamic tool approval for "human in the loop" systems, enhanced React hooks with onToolCall callback, and improved error handling for streaming responses.

## Starter Template

The starter kit includes a Chat UI built with Kumo components, streaming responses powered by Workers AI via AIChatAgent, three tool patterns (server-side auto-execute, client-side browser, and human-in-the-loop approval), scheduling capabilities, and WebSocket connection with automatic reconnection and message persistence.

Based on the search results, I found information about the `@cloudflare/ai-chat` package and the `useAgentChat` hook in the Cloudflare Agents changelog. Here's what I found:

## @cloudflare/ai-chat and useAgentChat

The first stable release of @cloudflare/ai-chat ships alongside Agents SDK v0.5.0 with a major refactor of AIChatAgent internals — new ResumableStream class, WebSocket ChatTransport, and simplified SSE parsing — with zero breaking changes. Existing code using AIChatAgent and useAgentChat works as-is.

# @cloudflare/ai-chat v0.1.0 Release Summary

## Overview
This represents "the first stable release of `@cloudflare/ai-chat`" with a comprehensive internal restructuring that maintains backward compatibility.

## Key Changes
**Internal Refactoring (Zero Breaking Changes):**
- New `ResumableStream` class
- WebSocket `ChatTransport` implementation
- Simplified SSE parsing logic

Existing code using `AIChatAgent` and `useAgentChat` "works as-is" without modifications.

## Major New Features

**1. Data Parts**
Attach typed JSON objects to messages alongside text content. Supports reconciliation (in-place type+id updates), append operations, and transient parts via `onData` callbacks.

**2. Tool Approval Persistence**
The approval UI now "survives page refresh and DO hibernation" by persisting streaming messages to SQLite when tools enter `approval-requested` state.

**3. Message Storage Controls**
- `maxPersistedMessages` option caps SQLite storage with automatic deletion of oldest messages
- Hash-based caching prevents redundant SQL writes
- Automatic two-pass compaction when approaching SQLite's 2 MB row limit

**4. Enhanced useAgentChat**
New `body` option sends custom data with every request (static or dynamic).

**5. Automatic Tool Continuation**
`autoContinueAfterToolResult` now defaults to `true`, automatically triggering server continuation after client-side tool results and approvals.

## Notable Bug Fixes
- Stream resumption race conditions resolved
- Client tool schemas now persist after Durable Object hibernation
- Message metadata properly propagated on broadcast/resume paths
- `reasoning-delta` data preservation during stream resumption

## Installation
```
npm i agents@latest @cloudflare/ai-chat@latest
```

# Cloudflare Agents Starter Kit Analysis

## Project Overview
This is a TypeScript-based starter template for building AI chat agents on Cloudflare's infrastructure. It demonstrates three tool execution patterns and real-time WebSocket communication.

## Core Architecture

### Key Dependencies
The stack uses:
- **AI Framework**: `@ai-sdk/[provider]` (Workers AI by default, supports OpenAI/Anthropic)
- **Agent Base**: `AIChatAgent` class from the Agents SDK
- **Frontend**: React with Kumo UI components
- **Runtime**: Cloudflare Workers with Durable Objects

### Integration Pattern

**Server-side (server.ts)**:
```
AIChatAgent extends class → implements onChatMessage() 
→ uses streamText() with tools object → manages message history
```

**Client-side (app.tsx)**:
```
useAgentChat hook → manages WebSocket connection → handles tool callbacks
```

## Three Tool Execution Patterns

1. **Server-side auto-execute**: Tool with `execute` function runs automatically without user interaction
2. **Client-side**: Tool without `execute` function; browser provides result via `onToolCall` callback
3. **Human-in-the-loop**: Tool includes `needsApproval` gate; executes after approval

## Project Structure
```
src/
  server.ts    # AIChatAgent subclass with tool definitions
  app.tsx      # React UI with useAgentChat hook
  client.tsx   # Entry point
  styles.css   # Tailwind + Kumo styling
```

## Key Features Demonstrated

- **Streaming responses** via Workers AI (no API key required)
- **Scheduling**: One-time, delayed, and cron tasks with `executeTask()` method
- **State management**: `this.setState()` and `this.state` for real-time synchronization
- **Callable RPC methods**: `@callable()` decorator for direct client invocation
- **MCP server integration**: Connect external tool providers via `this.mcp.connect()`
- **Message persistence**: SQLite-backed conversation history
- **Debug mode**: Toggle to inspect raw message JSON

## Customization Entry Points

**System prompt modification**: Edit the `system` string in server.ts  
**Tool replacement**: Replace demo implementations (weather, calculate) with real API calls  
**Task execution**: Override `executeTask()` method for custom scheduling behavior  
**Model switching**: Change import to `@ai-sdk/anthropic` or `@ai-sdk/openai`; add API keys to `.env`

## Deployment
The starter runs on Cloudflare's global network via `npm run deploy`. Workers automatically hibernate when idle and resume stream connections on client reconnection.

# Chat Agents API Reference - Complete Guide

## Installation & Imports

```bash
npm install @cloudflare/ai-chat agents ai
```

**Import paths:**
- `AIChatAgent`: `@cloudflare/ai-chat`
- `useAgentChat`: `@cloudflare/ai-chat/react`
- Types: `@cloudflare/ai-chat/types`

---

## AIChatAgent Class API

### Class Definition
Server-side agent that extends `Agent` from the agents package. "Manages conversation state, persistence, and streaming."

### Constructor & Properties

```typescript
export class ChatAgent extends AIChatAgent {
  // Access current conversation messages
  messages: UIMessage[]
  
  // Cap messages stored in SQLite
  maxPersistedMessages = 200;
  
  // Control MCP connection waiting
  waitForMcpConnections = { timeout: 10_000 };
}
```

**`maxPersistedMessages`** (optional)
- Type: `number`
- Default: unbounded
- When exceeded, oldest messages are deleted from storage only (doesn't affect LLM context)

**`waitForMcpConnections`** (optional)
- `{ timeout: 10_000 }` — wait up to 10 seconds (default)
- `true` — wait indefinitely
- `false` — do not wait

### Core Methods

#### `onChatMessage(onFinish?, options?)`

Main override method. Must return a `Response` object.

**Parameters:**
- `onFinish?`: Optional callback for `streamText` cleanup (automatic)
- `options?`: Object containing:
  - `abortSignal`: Cancel signal for request cancellation
  - `body`: Custom data from client
  - `requestId`: Unique identifier for logging

**Return value:** `Response` (streaming or plain text)

**Streaming response example:**
```typescript
async onChatMessage() {
  const workersai = createWorkersAI({ binding: this.env.AI });
  const result = streamText({
    model: workersai("@cf/zai-org/glm-4.7-flash"),
    system: "You are helpful.",
    messages: await convertToModelMessages(this.messages),
  });
  return result.toUIMessageStreamResponse();
}
```

**Plain text response example:**
```typescript
async onChatMessage() {
  return new Response("Hello! I am a simple agent.", {
    headers: { "Content-Type": "text/plain" },
  });
}
```

**Accessing custom body data:**
```typescript
async onChatMessage(_onFinish, options) {
  const { timezone, userId } = options?.body ?? {};
  console.log("Request ID:", options?.requestId);
}
```

#### `persistMessages(messages)`
Persist messages without triggering a response.

```typescript
await this.persistMessages(messages);
```

#### `saveMessages(messages)`
Persist messages AND trigger `onChatMessage` (for programmatic messages).

```typescript
await this.saveMessages(messages);
```

#### `destroy()`
Cancels pending chat requests and cleans up stream state. Called automatically on Durable Object eviction.

### Lifecycle Hooks

#### `onConnect(connection, ctx)`
Called when a client connects.

```typescript
async onConnect(connection, ctx) {
  console.log("Client connected:", connection.id);
  // Stream resumption and sync handled automatically
}
```

#### `onClose(connection, code, reason, wasClean)`
Called when a client disconnects.

```typescript
async onClose(connection, code, reason, wasClean) {
  console.log("Client disconnected:", connection.id);
  // Cleanup handled automatically
}
```

### Request Cancellation

Forward `abortSignal` from options to `streamText` for cancellation support:

```typescript
async onChatMessage(_onFinish, options) {
  const result = streamText({
    model: workersai("@cf/zai-org/glm-4.7-flash"),
    messages: await convertToModelMessages(this.messages),
    abortSignal: options?.abortSignal, // Enable cancellation
  });
  return result.toUIMessageStreamResponse();
}
```

---

## useAgentChat Hook API

React hook connecting to `AIChatAgent` over WebSocket.

### Basic Usage
```typescript
import { useAgent } from "agents/react";
import { useAgentChat } from "@cloudflare/ai-chat/react";

function Chat() {
  const agent = useAgent({ agent: "ChatAgent" });
  const { messages, sendMessage, status } = useAgentChat({ agent });
  // ...
}
```

### Props/Options

| Option | Type | Default | Description |
|--------|------|---------|-------------|
| `agent` | `ReturnType<typeof useAgent>` | Required | Agent connection |
| `onToolCall` | `({ toolCall, addToolOutput }) => void` | — | Handle client-side tools |
| `autoContinueAfterToolResult` | `boolean` | `true` | Auto-continue after tool results |
| `resume` | `boolean` | `true` | Enable stream resumption |
| `body` | `object \| () => object` | — | Custom data per request |
| `prepareSendMessagesRequest` | `(options) => { body?, headers? }` | — | Per-request customization |
| `getInitialMessages` | `(options) => Promise<UIMessage[]> \| null` | — | Custom message loader |
| `onData` | `(part) => void` | — | Handle transient data parts |

### Return Values

| Property | Type | Description |
|----------|------|-------------|
| `messages` | `UIMessage[]` | Current conversation |
| `sendMessage` | `(message) => void` | Send a message |
| `clearHistory` | `() => void` | Clear all messages |
| `addToolOutput` | `({ toolCallId, output }) => void` | Provide tool output |
| `addToolApprovalResponse` | `({ id, approved }) => void` | Approve/reject tool |
| `setMessages` | `(messages \| updater) => void` | Set messages directly |
| `status` | `string` | "idle", "submitted", "streaming", "error" |

### Custom Body Data

**Static:**
```typescript
const { messages, sendMessage } = useAgentChat({
  agent,
  body: {
    timezone: Intl.DateTimeFormat().resolvedOptions().timeZone,
    userId: currentUser.id,
  },
});
```

**Dynamic:**
```typescript
body: () => ({
  token: getAuthToken(),
  timestamp: Date.now(),
})
```

**Per-request customization:**
```typescript
prepareSendMessagesRequest: async ({ messages, trigger }) => ({
  headers: { Authorization: `Bearer ${await getToken()}` },
  body: { requestedAt: Date.now() },
})
```

---

## Message Persistence

### How It Works

"Messages are automatically persisted to SQLite, survive restarts."

**Flow:**
1. Client sends message via WebSocket
2. `AIChatAgent` persists to SQLite and calls `onChatMessage`
3. Returns streaming `Response`
4. Chunks stream over WebSocket in real-time
5. Final message persisted and broadcast to all connections

### Storage Management

**Row size protection:** "When a message approaches SQLite's 2 MB limit, automatic compaction occurs."

- Large tool outputs replaced with LLM-friendly summary
- Text truncated if still oversized
- Includes `metadata.compactedToolOutputs` for client detection

### Controlling Storage vs LLM Context

| Aspect | Control | Scope |
|--------|---------|-------|
| Messages in SQLite | `maxPersistedMessages` | Persistence |
| What model sees | `pruneMessages()` | LLM context |
| Row size limits | Automatic | Per-message |

**Example:**
```typescript
const result = streamText({
  model: workersai("@cf/zai-org/glm-4.7-flash"),
  messages: pruneMessages({
    messages: await convertToModelMessages(this.messages),
    reasoning: "before-last-message",
    toolCalls: "before-last-2-messages",
  }),
});
```

---

## Streaming & Resumption

### How Streaming Works

"Streams automatically resume when a client disconnects and reconnects."

**Process:**
1. All chunks buffered in SQLite during generation
2. If client disconnects, server continues streaming/buffering
3. Client reconnects and receives buffered chunks
4. Resumes live streaming

**Disable resumption:**
```typescript
const { messages } = useAgentChat({ agent, resume: false });
```

### Data Parts (Structured Data)

Attach typed JSON alongside text using `createUIMessageStream`:

**Server-side writing:**
```typescript
const stream = createUIMessageStream({
  execute: async ({ writer }) => {
    const result = streamText({
      model: workersai("@cf/zai-org/glm-4.7-flash"),
      messages: await convertToModelMessages(this.messages),
    });
    writer.merge(result.toUIMessageStream());
    
    // Write data part
    writer.write({
      type: "data-sources",
      id: "src-1",
      data: { query: "agents", status: "searching", results: [] },
    });
    
    // Update same part in-place
    writer.write({
      type: "data-sources",
      id: "src-1",
      data: {
        query: "agents",
        status: "found",
        results: ["Agents SDK docs", "Durable Objects guide"],
      },
    });
  },
});
return createUIMessageStreamResponse({ stream });
```

**Three patterns:**

| Pattern | Method | Persisted | Use Case |
|---------|--------|-----------|----------|
| Reconciliation | Same `type` + `id` updates in-place | Yes | Progressive state |
| Append | Different id or no id | Yes | Multiple citations |
| Transient | `transient: true` | No | Ephemeral status |

**Client-side reading:**
```typescript
type ChatMessage = UIMessage<
  unknown,
  {
    sources: { query: string; status: string; results: string[] };
  }
>;

const { messages } = useAgentChat<unknown, ChatMessage>({ agent });

for (const msg of messages) {
  for (const part of msg.parts) {
    if (part.type === "data-sources") {
      console.log(part.data.results); // string[]
    }
  }
}
```

**Transient parts (use `onData` callback):**
```typescript
const [thinking, setThinking] = useState(false);

const { messages } = useAgentChat({
  agent,
  onData(part) {
    if (part.type === "data-thinking") {
      setThinking(true);
    }
  },
});
```

---

## Tools

### Three Tool Patterns

| Pattern | Where | When |
|---------|-------|------|
| Server-side | Server (automatic) | API, database, compute |
| Client-side | Browser (via `onToolCall`) | Geolocation, clipboard, camera |
| Approval | Server (after user approval) | Payments, deletions |

### Server-Side Tools

Tools with `execute` run automatically:

```typescript
const result = streamText({
  model: workersai("@cf/zai-org/glm-4.7-flash"),
  messages: await convertToModelMessages(this.messages),
  tools: {
    getWeather: tool({
      description: "Get weather for a city",
      inputSchema: z.object({ city: z.string() }),
      execute: async ({ city }) => {
        const data = await fetchWeather(city);
        return { temperature: data.temp, condition: data.condition };
      },
    }),
  },
});
```

### Client-Side Tools

Define on server without `execute`, handle on client:

**Server:**
```typescript
tools: {
  getLocation: tool({
    description: "Get the user's location from the browser",
    inputSchema: z.object({}),
    // No execute — client handles it
  }),
}
```

**Client:**
```typescript
const { messages, sendMessage } = useAgentChat({
  agent,
  onToolCall: async ({ toolCall, addToolOutput }) => {
    if (toolCall.toolName === "getLocation") {
      const pos = await new Promise((resolve, reject) =>
        navigator.geolocation.getCurrentPosition(resolve, reject),
      );
      addToolOutput({
        toolCallId: toolCall.toolCallId,
        output: { lat: pos.coords.latitude, lng: pos.coords.longitude },
      });
    }
  },
});
```

### Tool Approval (Human-in-the-Loop)

**Server (define approval condition):**
```typescript
tools: {
  processPayment: tool({
    description: "Process a payment",
    inputSchema: z.object({
      amount: z.number(),
      recipient: z.string(),
    }),
    needsApproval: async ({ amount }) => amount > 100,
    execute: async ({ amount, recipient }) => charge(amount, recipient),
  }),
}
```

**Client (render approval UI):**
```typescript
const { messages, addToolApprovalResponse } = useAgentChat({ agent });

{
  messages.map((msg) =>
    msg.parts
      .filter((part) => part.type === "tool" && part.state === "approval-required")
      .map((part) => (
        <div key={part.toolCallId}>
          <p>Approve {part.toolName}?</p>
          <button
            onClick={() =>
              addToolApprovalResponse({
                id: part.toolCallId,
                approved: true,
              })
            }
          >
            Approve
          </button>
          <button
            onClick={() =>
              addToolApprovalResponse({
                id: part.toolCallId,
                approved: false,
              })
            }
          >
            Reject
          </button>
        </div>
      )),
  );
}
```

**Custom denial messages:**
```typescript
const { addToolOutput } = useAgentChat({ agent });

addToolOutput({
  toolCallId: part.toolCallId,
  state: "output-error",
  errorText: "User declined: insufficient budget for this quarter",
});
```

---

## Wrangler Configuration

```json
{
  "ai": { "binding": "AI" },
  "durable_objects": {
    "bindings": [{ "name": "ChatAgent", "class_name": "ChatAgent" }]
  },
  "migrations": [{ "tag": "v1", "new_sqlite_classes": ["ChatAgent"] }]
}
```

"The `new_sqlite_classes` migration is required — `AIChatAgent` uses SQLite for message persistence and stream chunk buffering."

---

## Multi-Client Sync

When multiple clients connect to same agent instance, "messages are automatically broadcast to all connections."

- Originating client receives streaming response
- Other clients receive final messages via broadcast
- All clients stay synchronized automatically

---

## WebSocket Protocol

| Message | Direction | Purpose |
|---------|-----------|---------|
| `CF_AGENT_USE_CHAT_REQUEST` | Client → Server | Send chat message |
| `CF_AGENT_USE_CHAT_RESPONSE` | Server → Client | Stream chunks |
| `CF_AGENT_CHAT_MESSAGES` | Server → Client | Broadcast updates |
| `CF_AGENT_CHAT_CLEAR` | Bidirectional | Clear conversation |
| `CF_AGENT_CHAT_REQUEST_CANCEL` | Client → Server | Cancel stream |
| `CF_AGENT_TOOL_RESULT` | Client → Server | Provide tool output |
| `CF_AGENT_TOOL_APPROVAL` | Client → Server | Approve/reject tool |

---

## Provider Examples

### Workers AI
```typescript
import { createWorkersAI } from "workers-ai-provider";

const workersai = createWorkersAI({ binding: this.env.AI });
const result = streamText({
  model: workersai("@cf/zai-org/glm-4.7-flash"),
  messages: await convertToModelMessages(this.messages),
});
```

### OpenAI
```typescript
import { createOpenAI } from "@ai-sdk/openai";

const openai = createOpenAI({ apiKey: this.env.OPENAI_API_KEY });
const result = streamText({
  model: openai.chat("gpt-4o"),
  messages: await convertToModelMessages(this.messages),
});
```

### Anthropic
```typescript
import { createAnthropic } from "@ai-sdk/anthropic";

const anthropic = createAnthropic({ apiKey: this.env.ANTHROPIC_API_KEY });
const result = streamText({
  model: anthropic("claude-sonnet-4-20250514"),
  messages: await convertToModelMessages(this.messages),
});

## Deprecated APIs

These will be removed in a future release:

| Deprecated | Replacement |
|-----------|-------------|
| `addToolResult({ toolCallId, result })` | `addToolOutput({ toolCallId, output })` |
| `createToolsFromClientSchemas()` | Client tools auto-registered |
| `extractClientToolSchemas()` | Schemas sent with tool results |
| `detectToolsRequiringConfirmation()` | Use `needsApproval` on tool |
| `tools` option on `useAgentChat` | Define tools in `onChatMessage` |
| `toolsRequiringConfirmation` option | Use `needsApproval` per-tool |
```

## `@cloudflare/ai-chat` — Cloudflare の Chat SDK

前回の回答で `agents/ai-react` と言っていましたが、**最新のパッケージは `@cloudflare/ai-chat`** に変わっています（2026年2月リリースの v0.1.0）。今のプロジェクトの `agents` パッケージからの import は deprecated になる方向です。

`★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────`
- **`@cloudflare/ai-chat`** は Vercel AI SDK の `useChat` を WebSocket transport で wrap したもの。つまり「Vercel の Chat SDK を使えばいいの？」という質問の答えは「**内部的には使っている。Cloudflare 版ラッパーを使う**」が正解
- 今の `agents/src/agent.ts` の import パス（`agents/ai-chat-agent`）は deprecated 予定。`@cloudflare/ai-chat` に移行すべき
`─────────────────────────────────────────────────`

### 今のコードとの差分

```typescript
// ❌ 現在のコード (deprecated path)
import { AIChatAgent } from "agents/ai-chat-agent";

// ✅ 新しいパッケージ
import { AIChatAgent } from "@cloudflare/ai-chat";
```

```typescript
// フロントエンド側
import { useAgent } from "agents/react";
import { useAgentChat } from "@cloudflare/ai-chat/react";
```

### このSDKが提供するもの

| 機能 | 説明 |
|------|------|
| **メッセージ永続化** | SQLite に自動保存。リロードしても会話が残る |
| **ストリーム再開** | 切断→再接続しても途中からストリーミング再開 |
| **3種類のツール** | サーバー自動実行 / クライアント側 / 承認フロー付き |
| **Data Parts** | テキスト以外の構造化データ（グラフ、進捗等）をメッセージに添付 |
| **マルチクライアント同期** | 同じエージェントに複数クライアント接続→自動ブロードキャスト |
| **ストレージ制御** | `maxPersistedMessages` で上限設定、自動コンパクション |

### 今のプロジェクトに適用する場合のイメージ

**バックエンド（`agent.ts` — ほぼそのまま）**:
```typescript
import { AIChatAgent } from "@cloudflare/ai-chat";
import { createWorkersAI } from "workers-ai-provider";

export class DataAgent extends AIChatAgent<Env> {
  async onChatMessage(_onFinish, options) {
    const workersai = createWorkersAI({ binding: this.env.AI });
    const result = streamText({
      model: workersai(MODEL_ID),
      system: SYSTEM_PROMPT,
      messages: await convertToModelMessages(this.messages),
      tools: createTools(this.env.DB),
      abortSignal: options?.abortSignal,  // キャンセル対応
    });
    return result.toUIMessageStreamResponse();
  }
}
```

**フロントエンド（新規）**:
```typescript
import { useAgent } from "agents/react";
import { useAgentChat } from "@cloudflare/ai-chat/react";

function Chat() {
  const agent = useAgent({ agent: "data-agent" });
  const { messages, sendMessage, status, clearHistory } = useAgentChat({ agent });

  return (
    <div>
      {messages.map(msg => (
        <div key={msg.id}>{/* メッセージ表示 */}</div>
      ))}
      <input onKeyDown={e => {
        if (e.key === "Enter") sendMessage(e.currentTarget.value);
      }} />
    </div>
  );
}
```

### 特に面白い機能

**1. Human-in-the-Loop（承認フロー付きツール）**

今のエージェントは全クエリを自動実行していますが、`needsApproval` を付けるとユーザー承認を挟める:

```typescript
queryD1: tool({
  inputSchema: z.object({ sql: z.string() }),
  needsApproval: async ({ sql }) => sql.includes("DELETE"),  // 条件付き承認
  execute: async ({ sql }) => { /* ... */ },
}),
```

**2. クライアント側ツール**

ブラウザの位置情報やクリップボードなど、サーバーからは取れないデータをツールとして定義できる。

### 次のステップとして

1. **`@cloudflare/ai-chat` への移行**（import パス変更 + パッケージ追加）
2. **React フロントエンドの追加**（Cloudflare Pages or Workers Static Assets）
3. **承認フロー付きツールの検討**（書き込み操作が増えた時に有用）

どこから手を付けたいですか？

# Chat SDK Overview

## What It Is
Chat SDK is a unified TypeScript SDK for building chatbots across multiple messaging platforms. According to the documentation, it's "A unified TypeScript SDK for building chat bots across Slack, Microsoft Teams, Google Chat, Discord, and more."

## Creator
Vercel developed Chat SDK, as indicated by the branding and copyright notice: "Copyright Vercel 2026."

## Key Features

**Multi-Platform Support**
Developers can deploy to Slack, Teams, Google Chat, Discord, GitHub, and Linear from a single codebase.

**Type Safety**
The SDK includes "Full TypeScript support with type-safe adapters, event handlers, and JSX cards."

**AI Streaming**
It provides "First-class support for streaming LLM responses with native platform rendering."

**Event-Driven Architecture**
The SDK is designed around real-time event handling with handlers for mentions, reactions, message subscriptions, and platform-specific events.

## Core Components
- Platform adapters (Slack, Teams, Discord, etc.)
- State adapters (Redis, distributed locking, caching)
- Thread and message management
- Interactive cards with buttons and forms
- Slash commands and modals
- Ephemeral messaging
- File uploads

## Technology Stack
- TypeScript
- Next.js (for example implementations)
- Redis (for state management)
- Supports Hono and Nuxt frameworks

## Documentation & Resources
- **GitHub Repository**: https://github.com/vercel/chat/
- **Documentation**: /docs section with guides, API reference, and adapter documentation
- **Getting Started**: https://chat.ai-sdk.dev/

## Status
The project is described as "now open source and in beta."

# Chat SDK Architecture Overview

## Project Description
The Chat SDK is a unified TypeScript framework enabling developers to "Write your bot logic once, deploy everywhere" across multiple chat platforms with a single codebase.

## Core Architecture

**Main Components:**
- **Core Package** (`chat`): Provides the `Chat` class, types, JSX runtime, and utilities
- **Platform Adapters**: Modular packages for each supported service
- **State Adapters**: Persistence layer supporting Redis, PostgreSQL, ioredis, and in-memory options

## Supported Platforms

| Platform | Adapter Package | Key Features |
|----------|---|---|
| Slack | `@chat-adapter/slack` | Native streaming, modals, full reactions |
| Microsoft Teams | `@chat-adapter/teams` | Post+Edit streaming, read-only reactions |
| Google Chat | `@chat-adapter/gchat` | Post+Edit streaming, reactions |
| Discord | `@chat-adapter/discord` | Post+Edit streaming, reactions |
| Telegram | `@chat-adapter/telegram` | Post+Edit streaming, partial cards |
| GitHub | `@chat-adapter/github` | Reactions, mentions only |
| Linear | `@chat-adapter/linear` | Reactions, mentions only |
| WhatsApp | `@chat-adapter/whatsapp` | DMs, partial cards |

## Code Example Structure

```typescript
// Initialize bot with adapter and state management
const bot = new Chat({
  userName: "mybot",
  adapters: { slack: createSlackAdapter() },
  state: createRedisState(),
});

// Event handlers for bot behavior
bot.onNewMention(async (thread) => {
  await thread.subscribe();
  await thread.post("Message content");
});

bot.onSubscribedMessage(async (thread, message) => {
  // Process incoming messages
});
```

## Key Features Implemented

- Event handling (mentions, messages, reactions, commands)
- AI streaming with platform-specific fallbacks
- JSX-based interactive card components
- Modal dialogs with validation
- File attachment support
- Direct message initiation
- Ephemeral messaging capabilities

## Development Stack

- **Language**: TypeScript (92.5% of codebase)
- **Build Tool**: Turbo monorepo
- **Package Manager**: pnpm
- **Testing**: Vitest
- **Code Quality**: Biome

## Repository Structure

```
packages/          # Core & adapter implementations
apps/docs/         # Documentation site
examples/          # Sample implementations
skills/chat/       # AI coding agent integration
```

**Repository Metrics**: 1.2K stars, 83 forks, 472 commits, 632 dependents

# Chat SDK Overview

## Core Architecture

Chat SDK provides a unified TypeScript library for multi-platform chatbot development. The system has three main components:

1. **Chat** - Central orchestrator managing adapters and event routing
2. **Adapters** - Platform-specific implementations handling webhooks, message formatting, and API calls
3. **State** - Pluggable persistence layer for thread subscriptions and distributed locking

## How It Works

The SDK abstracts platform differences behind a unified interface. Instead of maintaining separate codebases for each platform, you write bot logic once and deploy across Slack, Teams, Google Chat, Discord, Telegram, GitHub, Linear, and WhatsApp.

**Key benefits:**
- Single codebase for all platforms
- Type-safe adapters with full TypeScript support
- Event-driven architecture (mentions, reactions, button clicks, slash commands, modals)
- Thread subscriptions for multi-turn conversations
- JSX-based cards rendering natively on each platform
- First-class AI streaming support
- Serverless-ready with Redis state and message deduplication

## Adapter Pattern (Slack Example)

Adapters auto-detect credentials from environment variables (`SLACK_BOT_TOKEN`, `SLACK_SIGNING_SECRET`, `REDIS_URL`, etc.), enabling zero-config startup:

```typescript
const bot = new Chat({
  userName: "mybot",
  adapters: {
    slack: createSlackAdapter(),
  },
  state: createRedisState(),
});

bot.onNewMention(async (thread) => {
  await thread.subscribe();
  await thread.post("Hello! I'm listening to this thread.");
});
```

## Supported Platforms & Feature Matrix

| Platform | Mentions | Reactions | Cards | Modals | Streaming | DMs |
|----------|----------|-----------|-------|--------|-----------|-----|
| Slack | Yes | Yes | Yes | Yes | Native | Yes |
| Teams | Yes | Read-only | Yes | No | Post+Edit | Yes |
| Discord | Yes | Yes | Yes | No | Post+Edit | Yes |
| Telegram | Yes | Yes | Partial | No | Post+Edit | Yes |

## AI Streaming Implementation

Chat SDK provides native streaming support. For Slack, this means real-time LLM responses update in-place. Other platforms use post-and-edit patterns. The SDK handles platform differences transparently—you write streaming code once.

## Manual vs. SDK Comparison

**Building manually:** Learn each platform's API, handle webhook parsing, implement message formatting per platform, manage state separately, handle edge cases individually.

**With Chat SDK:** Unified event handlers, automatic platform abstraction, standardized thread management, built-in state persistence options (Redis, PostgreSQL, in-memory).

## Hono/Cloudflare Workers Integration

The SDK works with serverless frameworks. Use Hono as your HTTP handler, point webhooks to your Cloudflare Worker, and Chat SDK processes events. State adapters (Redis via Upstash, PostgreSQL via Neon) provide distributed persistence. The pattern keeps deployment lightweight while maintaining multi-platform support.

## Available Packages

**Core:** `chat` (SDK, JSX runtime, utilities)

**Adapters:** `@chat-adapter/slack`, `@chat-adapter/teams`, `@chat-adapter/gchat`, `@chat-adapter/discord`, `@chat-adapter/telegram`, `@chat-adapter/github`, `@chat-adapter/linear`, `@chat-adapter/whatsapp`

**State:** `@chat-adapter/state-redis` (production), `@chat-adapter/state-pg`, `@chat-adapter/state-memory` (dev)
```

> AGENT

なるほど、**Vercel の Chat SDK** ですね。これは前回話していた `@cloudflare/ai-chat`（Chat UI用）とは全く別物です。

`★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────`
- **Chat SDK** = 「**メッセージングプラットフォーム統合SDK**」。Slack, Teams, Discord, Telegram 等を1つのコードベースで扱う
- **`@cloudflare/ai-chat`** = 「**WebブラウザでのChat UI SDK**」。WebSocket + Durable Object でのリアルタイムチャット
- つまり解決する問題が違う。Chat SDK は「**どこからメッセージが来ても同じロジックで処理する**」ためのもの
`─────────────────────────────────────────────────`

## 今のプロジェクトとの関係

現在の `agents/src/slack.ts` は Slack API を直接叩いている手書きのコードです：

```
今の構成:
  slack.ts → fetch("https://slack.com/api/chat.postMessage", ...) を直接呼ぶ
  署名検証も手書き、レスポンス解析も手書き
```

Chat SDK を使うと：

```typescript
import { Chat } from "chat";
import { createSlackAdapter } from "@chat-adapter/slack";

const bot = new Chat({
  userName: "oura-agent",
  adapters: {
    slack: createSlackAdapter(),  // 署名検証、イベント解析を自動化
  },
  state: createRedisState(),     // スレッド状態の永続化
});

// これだけで Slack のメンションに応答
bot.onNewMention(async (thread) => {
  await thread.subscribe();
  // SQL生成 → 実行 → 要約 のパイプライン
  const answer = await queryAndSummarize(thread.lastMessage.text, env);
  await thread.post(answer);
});

// スレッド内の追加質問にも対応
bot.onSubscribedMessage(async (thread, message) => {
  const answer = await queryAndSummarize(message.text, env);
  await thread.post(answer);
});
```

## 使うべきか？

### 使うメリット
| 観点 | 現状（手書き） | Chat SDK |
|------|-------------|----------|
| Slack署名検証 | 自前実装（30行） | 自動 |
| エラーハンドリング | PR で指摘されて追加した | 組み込み |
| スレッド管理 | `thread_ts` を手動管理 | `thread.subscribe()` で自動 |
| AI ストリーミング | 未対応（全文完成後に投稿） | Slack ネイティブストリーミング対応 |
| 他プラットフォーム追加 | 各APIを一から実装 | アダプター追加のみ |

### 注意点
- **Redis が必要** — スレッド状態管理に Redis（Upstash 等）を使う。Cloudflare だけで完結しなくなる
- **Hono との統合** — Cloudflare Workers + Hono で動かす場合の具体的な事例がまだ少ない（beta）
- **現状のシンプルさ** — 今は Slack 1プラットフォームだけなので、オーバースペックになる可能性がある

### 判断のポイント

**今すぐ導入すべき場合:**
- Discord や Teams にも展開したい
- Slack でのストリーミング応答が欲しい
- スレッド内の会話を追跡したい

**まだ早い場合:**
- 当面 Slack だけで十分
- Redis 等の外部依存を増やしたくない
- まずはエージェントの機能充実が優先

個人的には、**今は機能充実を優先して、2つ目のプラットフォーム（Discord 等）を追加したくなったタイミングで Chat SDK に移行する**のが良いと思います。今のコード量なら移行コストも低いです。

Based on the search results, here's what I found about Cloudflare Workers, Redis, and Upstash in 2025-2026:

## Integration and Availability

Cloudflare has released the Upstash Integration for Cloudflare Workers, making it easier to use Upstash products on Workers. Cloudflare now offers three new integrations: Upstash Redis, Upstash Kafka and Upstash QStash, and these are available for all users.

## What Upstash Offers

Upstash is a serverless database with Redis and Kafka API. Upstash is a serverless data platform designed for modern cloud-native applications that offers managed Redis, Kafka, QStash (a serverless message queue), and a vector database, all with usage-based pricing and a "scale to zero" model.

## Key Advantages Over Cloudflare KV

While Cloudflare KV provides only basic get/set/delete functionality, Upstash gives you all Redis data structures where you can build much more sophisticated functionality including Hashes, Lists, SortedSets, ranges, appends, increments, etc. Cloudflare Workers KV has a hard limit that you can send 1 write operation to the same key per second, while Upstash Redis does not have such a limit.

## Connection Method

The Upstash Redis client is specifically designed to work with serverless and edge environments like Cloudflare Workers through its HTTP-based approach instead of traditional TCP connections, making it an ideal solution for stateful operations in Cloudflare Workers.


REMINDER: You MUST include the sources above in your response to the user using markdown hyperlinks.

> AGENT

使えます。**[Upstash Redis](https://upstash.com/docs/redis/quickstarts/cloudflareworkers)** が Cloudflare Workers の公式統合として提供されています。

`★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────`
- 通常の Redis は TCP 接続が必要だが、Workers は TCP を直接使えない。Upstash は **HTTP ベースの Redis クライアント** (`@upstash/redis`) を提供しており、Workers の `fetch` で動く
- Cloudflare ダッシュボードから直接 Upstash と統合でき、環境変数が自動セットされる
- 無料枠: 10,000 コマンド/日、256MB。Chat SDK のスレッド管理程度なら余裕
`─────────────────────────────────────────────────`

### Workers での使い方

```typescript
import { Redis } from "@upstash/redis/cloudflare";

const redis = Redis.fromEnv(env);  // UPSTASH_REDIS_REST_URL, UPSTASH_REDIS_REST_TOKEN
await redis.set("key", "value");
const val = await redis.get("key");
```

### ただし、本当に Redis が必要か？

Chat SDK は状態管理に Redis を使いますが、今のプロジェクトには**すでに同等のものが揃っています**：

| Chat SDK が Redis でやること | Cloudflare の代替 |
|---|---|
| スレッド購読状態の保持 | **KV** or **D1** |
| メッセージ重複排除 | **Durable Objects**（今使ってる） |
| 分散ロック | **Durable Objects**（単一インスタンス保証） |

Chat SDK を使う場合は Upstash を足せばいいし、使わずに自前で拡張するなら既存の D1/KV/DO で事足ります。どちらのパスでも問題ありません。

Sources:
- [Upstash × Cloudflare Workers (公式ドキュメント)](https://developers.cloudflare.com/workers/databases/third-party-integrations/upstash/)
- [Upstash Redis Quickstart for Workers](https://upstash.com/docs/redis/quickstarts/cloudflareworkers)

> DEVELOPER

durable objects って何？

> AGENT

Durable Objects は Cloudflare の**ステートフルなサーバーレスプリミティブ**です。今のプロジェクトの `DataAgent` がまさにこれです。

## 普通の Workers との違い

```
Workers（ステートレス）:
  リクエストA → Worker インスタンス① → レスポンス  ← 次のリクエストでは別インスタンスかも
  リクエストB → Worker インスタンス② → レスポンス  ← 前の状態を知らない

Durable Objects（ステートフル）:
  リクエストA → DO "user-123" → レスポンス  ← 常に同じインスタンス
  リクエストB → DO "user-123" → レスポンス  ← 前の状態を覚えている
```

**要するに「名前付きの、状態を持てる単一インスタンス」** です。

## なぜ「Durable」なのか

3つの永続性があります：

| 特性 | 意味 |
|------|------|
| **単一インスタンス保証** | 同じ ID の DO は世界中で1つしか存在しない |
| **SQLite 内蔵** | インスタンス内に SQLite DB を持てる（メッセージ履歴等） |
| **Hibernation** | アイドル時にメモリから退避し、リクエストが来たら復帰。状態は保持 |

## 今のプロジェクトでの使われ方

```typescript
// agents/src/agent.ts
export class DataAgent extends AIChatAgent<Env> {
  // this.messages → SQLite に自動永続化された会話履歴
  // WebSocket 接続も DO が管理
  async onChatMessage(onFinish) {
    const result = streamText({
      messages: await convertToModelMessages(this.messages), // ← 過去の会話を覚えてる
      tools: createTools(this.env.DB),
    });
    return result.toUIMessageStreamResponse();
  }
}
```

`★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────`
- `AIChatAgent` は内部で Durable Object を継承しており、会話の永続化・WebSocket 管理・ストリーム再開を全て DO の仕組みで実現している
- DO の「世界で1つのインスタンス」という保証は、分散ロックや排他制御を不要にする。チャットの整合性が自然に保たれる理由はここにある
- 対して `slack.ts` の方は通常の Worker として動いており、DO を経由していない。だから会話履歴を持てず、毎回ステートレスに処理している
`─────────────────────────────────────────────────`

## 身近なアナロジー

```
Workers     = コンビニのレジ（誰が対応するかわからない、前回の客を覚えてない）
Durable Objects = 担当制の窓口（「田中さん担当」は常に同じ人、過去のやりとりも記録済み）
```

今のプロジェクトだと、WebSocket 経由の Chat UI は DO（担当窓口）を使っていて会話を覚えるけど、Slack 側はレジ方式で毎回忘れる、という構造になっています。

oura ring のテーブルジョインしてくれるようになっている？

> AGENT

はい、すでに `v_oura_daily_summary` というビューでジョインされています。

マイグレーション（`infrastructure/d1/migrations/0002_oura_tables.sql:107-123`）で定義済みです：

```sql
CREATE VIEW IF NOT EXISTS v_oura_daily_summary AS
SELECT
    s.day,
    s.score AS sleep_score,
    a.score AS activity_score,
    r.score AS readiness_score,
    a.steps,
    a.total_calories,
    a.active_calories,
    r.temperature_deviation,
    s.deep_sleep AS sleep_deep_sleep,
    s.efficiency AS sleep_efficiency,
    s.total_sleep AS sleep_total_sleep
FROM oura_daily_sleep s
LEFT JOIN oura_daily_activity a ON s.day = a.day
LEFT JOIN oura_daily_readiness r ON s.day = r.day
ORDER BY s.day DESC;
```

### 構造

```
oura_daily_sleep ──┐
                   ├── LEFT JOIN on day ──→ v_oura_daily_summary
oura_daily_activity┤
                   │
oura_daily_readiness┘

oura_heart_rate ──→ (ビューに含まれていない)
```

### エージェントでの使われ方

`tools.ts` のシステムプロンプトで「**Prefer v_oura_daily_summary view**」と指示しているので、LLM は基本的にこのビューを使ってクエリを生成します。

ただし **2つの制限** があります：

1. **心拍数テーブル (`oura_heart_rate`) はビューに含まれていない** — 5分間隔の高頻度データなので日次集計と粒度が合わない
2. **ビューに含まれるカラムが限定的** — 例えば `activity` の `equivalent_walking_distance` や `training_volume` 等はビューにない

「昨日のスコア全部見せて」→ ビューで OK
「昨日の心拍数の推移は？」→ `oura_heart_rate` テーブルに直接クエリが必要

このあたり、拡張したいですか？

> DEVELOPER

データモデリングしたほうがいいのか

> AGENT

良い問いです。答えは「**今の規模ならまだ不要、だけどデータソースが増えるなら早めにやっておく価値がある**」です。

## 現状の整理

```
今のモデル: ほぼ raw テーブルそのまま

oura_daily_sleep      ← API レスポンスをそのまま格納
oura_daily_activity   ← 同上
oura_daily_readiness  ← 同上
oura_heart_rate       ← 同上
v_oura_daily_summary  ← 3テーブルを day で JOIN したビュー
```

これは **ELT の E(Extract) と L(Load) だけやって T(Transform) をビュー1つで済ませている** 状態です。

## データモデリングが必要になるタイミング

| シグナル | 今 | 将来 |
|---------|-----|------|
| データソース数 | Oura だけ | 天気、カレンダー、作業時間... |
| ユーザー数 | 自分だけ | 複数？ |
| 質問の複雑さ | 「昨日のスコアは？」 | 「雨の日は睡眠スコアが下がる？」 |
| テーブル数 | 5 | 10+？ |

**データソースが2つ以上になったら**モデリングの価値が出てきます。異なるソースのデータを「何で結合するか」が曖昧になるからです。

## やるならどのアプローチ？

### 案1: OBT（One Big Table）— おすすめ

```sql
-- fct_daily_health（日次の全指標を1行に）
CREATE VIEW fct_daily_health AS
SELECT
    s.day,
    -- Sleep
    s.score AS sleep_score,
    s.deep_sleep,
    s.efficiency AS sleep_efficiency,
    s.total_sleep,
    -- Activity
    a.score AS activity_score,
    a.steps,
    a.total_calories,
    a.active_calories,
    a.equivalent_walking_distance,
    -- Readiness
    r.score AS readiness_score,
    r.temperature_deviation,
    r.hrv_balance,
    r.resting_heart_rate,
    -- Heart Rate (日次集計)
    hr.avg_bpm,
    hr.min_bpm,
    hr.max_bpm,
    -- 将来: 天気
    -- w.temperature,
    -- w.pressure,
FROM oura_daily_sleep s
LEFT JOIN oura_daily_activity a ON s.day = a.day
LEFT JOIN oura_daily_readiness r ON s.day = r.day
LEFT JOIN (
    SELECT day, AVG(bpm) as avg_bpm, MIN(bpm) as min_bpm, MAX(bpm) as max_bpm
    FROM oura_heart_rate GROUP BY day
) hr ON s.day = hr.day;
```

`★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────`
- **OBT は AI エージェントとの相性が極めて良い**。LLM がJOINを書く必要がなくなり、1テーブルから全指標を取れるのでSQL生成の精度が上がる
- 今の `v_oura_daily_summary` は実質 OBT の簡易版。心拍数の日次集計と、activity の詳細カラムを足すだけで完成する
- dbt のセオリー（staging → marts）に従うなら `stg_oura__sleep`, `stg_oura__activity` → `fct_daily_health` という構成
`─────────────────────────────────────────────────`

### 案2: Star Schema（スタースキーマ）

```
           dim_dates
              │
fct_daily_health ─── dim_data_source
              │
         dim_users (将来)
```

**今は過剰**。ユーザーが自分だけ、データソースが Oura だけなら dimension が活きない。

### 案3: dbt で Transform 層を作る

今のプロジェクトには `transform/core/`（DuckDB）と `transform/fusion/`（Databricks）が既にあります。D1 用の dbt プロジェクトを作る…のは **D1 に dbt アダプターがないので現実的ではない**です。

代わりに、**D1 のマイグレーション内でビューを充実させる**のが Cloudflare ネイティブなやり方です。

## 推奨

```
今すぐ: v_oura_daily_summary を拡張して心拍数集計を追加（OBT化）
         → エージェントの回答精度が上がる

将来:   データソース追加時に staging ビュー + OBT の2層構成にする
         → stg_oura_sleep, stg_weather_daily → fct_daily_health
```

`★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────`
- `profiles.yml` に **R2 への接続設定が既に入っている**（`httpfs` + `parquet` extension、`s3_endpoint` に R2 を指定）
- prod プロファイルには `iceberg` extension まで入っている。つまり R2 上の Iceberg テーブルも読める構成
- 既存の dbt モデル（`stg_orders` → `fct_orders`, `dim_users` 等）がそのまま参考パターンになる
`─────────────────────────────────────────────────`

## やれること

```
Oura API → Worker → R2 (Parquet)  → dbt/DuckDB → モデリング済みデータ
                                         ↓
                                   fct_daily_health (OBT)
                                         ↓
                                   D1 に書き戻し or R2 に出力
                                         ↓
                                   エージェントが参照
```

### 具体的なフロー

**Step 1: Worker が Oura データを R2 に Parquet で保存**
```typescript
// 今: D1 に直接 INSERT
// 新: R2 に Parquet で保存（raw レイヤー）
await env.BUCKET.put(
  `raw/oura/daily_sleep/day=${day}/data.parquet`,
  parquetBuffer
);
```

**Step 2: dbt で staging → marts のモデリング**
```sql
-- transform/core/models/staging/oura/stg_oura__daily_sleep.sql
SELECT *
FROM read_parquet('s3://your-bucket/raw/oura/daily_sleep/**/*.parquet')

-- transform/core/models/marts/health/fct_daily_health.sql
SELECT
    s.day,
    s.sleep_score,
    a.activity_score,
    a.steps,
    r.readiness_score,
    hr.avg_bpm,
    -- 天気データも将来ここで JOIN
FROM {{ ref('stg_oura__daily_sleep') }} s
LEFT JOIN {{ ref('stg_oura__daily_activity') }} a ON s.day = a.day
LEFT JOIN {{ ref('stg_oura__daily_readiness') }} r ON s.day = r.day
LEFT JOIN {{ ref('stg_oura__heart_rate_daily') }} hr ON s.day = hr.day
```

**Step 3: 変換結果をエージェントに渡す**
- 方法A: dbt の結果を D1 に書き戻す（エージェントは今まで通り D1 クエリ）
- 方法B: dbt の結果を R2 に Parquet で出力し、エージェントが DuckDB WASM で直接読む

## 方法A vs 方法B

| | A: dbt → D1 に書き戻し | B: dbt → R2 のまま |
|---|---|---|
| エージェント変更 | 不要（今の D1 クエリのまま） | DuckDB WASM or Hyperdrive が必要 |
| リアルタイム性 | dbt 実行後に反映 | 同じ |
| 複雑さ | 低い | 高い |
| 柔軟性 | D1 の 10GB 制限 | R2 は 実質無制限 |

## 結論

**方法A（dbt → D1 書き戻し）が現実的**です。

1. R2 に raw データを Parquet で蓄積
2. dbt/DuckDB でモデリング（`fct_daily_health`）
3. 結果を D1 のビューまたはテーブルとして反映
4. エージェントは今まで通り D1 を参照

`profiles.yml` の R2 接続設定がもう入っているので、**dbt モデルを書くだけで始められる**状態です。Oura の staging モデルから作りますか？

Based on the web search results, here's what I found about DuckDB WASM, Cloudflare Workers, R2, and Parquet:

## Key Recent Developments (2025-2026)

Ducklings is a minimal DuckDB WASM build created specifically for browsers and serverless environments, making it possible to deploy DuckDB to Cloudflare Workers for the first time.

## Technical Capabilities

Emscripten's Asyncify is used to bridge DuckDB's synchronous HTTP calls to the Workers fetch() API, making httpfs work transparently. The @ducklings/workers package includes Parquet, JSON, and httpfs extensions compiled in statically.

## Cloudflare R2 Integration

To use DuckDB WASM on Cloudflare, you need to expose the R2 bucket via a custom domain. You can query remote Parquet, CSV, and JSON files directly through httpfs, and you can also access private S3-compatible storage (AWS S3, Cloudflare R2, Google Cloud Storage) using DuckDB secrets.

## Practical Example from 2025

Hosting data as static files in Cloudflare's R2 and querying with WASM-deployed DuckDB means you don't have to worry about a server at all – it should scale to however many people want to use the service, with the client's machine handling query creation and data display via JavaScript.

## Important Constraints

Workers have a 128 MB memory limit, the ~9.6 MB gzipped WASM binary requires a paid Workers plan ($5/month), while the free tier caps at 3 MB.

Here are the key findings regarding duckdb-wasm feasibility on Cloudflare Workers with memory limit considerations:

## Bundle Size Constraints

The main limitation is Cloudflare Workers' bundle size cap of 1 MB, while the gzipped duckdb-wasm module is around 1.8 MB, making it impossible to bundle the module alongside the worker script. However, a Cloudflare Workers Paid Plan is required due to the WASM size (~9.7MB), as the free plan has a 3MB limit while paid plans support up to 10MB.

## Memory Limitations

Workers and Durable Objects have a 128MB memory limit, and DuckDB operations can quickly exceed this. This is a fundamental constraint for handling larger datasets.

## Runtime Limitations

Workers are single-threaded by nature, and the WASM build has threading disabled, preventing parallel query execution. Additionally, DuckDB-WASM on Workers supports only in-memory databases with no persistent storage, with each new Worker instance starting fresh, requiring users to use httpfs to read external data or populate tables on init.

## Alternative Solutions

Ducklings makes it possible to run DuckDB at the edge on Cloudflare Workers using the @ducklings/workers package, which handles async I/O constraints, provides a TypeScript API, and includes httpfs, Parquet, and JSON extensions.

## Viable Use Cases

This enables use cases such as SQL APIs without a database server, edge data transformations, Parquet-to-JSON converters, and analytics endpoints that query directly from object storage.
